```
classify_pump(bc, cp):                       // bc = PDA(pump, ["bonding-curve", mint]); cp = canonical PumpSwap pool PDA
                                             // BOTH keys are always derived on-chain and required (key-checked) — C0-15
  if bc.owner == pump && bc.disc == BondingCurve:
      require !curve.is_mayhem_mode && curve.quote_mint == Pubkey::default()      // ADOPTED Q6
      require bc.data_len ≥ 115 (else CurveNeedsExtend; client prepends pump extend_account)
      if !curve.complete                        -> PumpCurve
      elif canonical_pool_valid(cp)             -> PumpSwap           (graduated_at = now; A12)
      elif curve.virtual_token_reserves > 0     -> PumpMigrating      (migration_seen_at = now)
      else                                      -> GraduatedElsewhere // complete, reserves 0, no canonical pool (e.g. Fartcoin [V3])
  else                                          -> NotPump

create_market(venue_hint ∈ {Auto, RaydiumV4, RaydiumCpmm}):
  k = classify_pump(bc, cp)
  if k ∈ {PumpCurve, PumpMigrating, PumpSwap}:  require venue_hint == Auto (else PumpVenuePrecedence; D1); venue = k
  elif venue_hint == Auto:                      venue = None     (graduated_elsewhere flag set if k == GraduatedElsewhere)
  else:                                         candidate accounts mandatory; require eligible(candidate) (§2.6);
                                                venue = hint; adopt candidate; graduated_at = age_start(candidate)

sync_venue (prologue of every instruction that carries a ReadSet):
  PumpCurve     : complete ? (cp valid ? PumpSwap : (vT > 0 ? PumpMigrating : None*)) : stay
  PumpMigrating : cp valid ? PumpSwap : (vT == 0 ? None* : stay)
  PumpSwap      : stay
  RaydiumV4/Cpmm: stay (changes only through the pool-selection path, §2.6)
  None          : crank_price re-runs classify_pump (always passed): pump venue found -> that venue (C0-15; M5 / M6);
                  a Raydium pool is adopted only through propose_pool (§2.6; never by crank_price):
                    open_positions == 0 : propose_pool adopts any eligible candidate at once (kind listing)
                    open_positions  > 0 : (M4-SEC-4; reachable from M5, *) propose_pool opens / improves a collection window
                                          of pool_fast_window_s (no arming: the market has no route at all), execute_pool_switch
                                          after it adopts the deepest candidate seen (kind fast), or cancels if the detection
                                          set now shows a pump venue (D1)
  * A live curve market whose curve completes with zeroed reserves and no canonical pool means pump changed its
    migration target: venue = None, graduated_elsewhere = 1, emit VenueStateChanged. The market has no route, so the
    S3 chain runs (§5.3); a Raydium pool can then be adopted through the collection window above (M5 reuses M4's path),
    never first-come: every later non-fast change of that pool needs open_positions == 0 (§2.6 rule 2).

canonical_pool_valid(cp) := cp.owner == pAMM && disc == Pool && pool.index == 0
    && pool.creator == PDA(pump, ["pool-authority", mint]) && pool.base_mint == mint
    && pool.quote_mint == WSOL && !pool.is_mayhem_mode                     (the address is derived, so it is unspoofable)

On → PumpSwap:
  cache pool_base_vault/pool_quote_vault (Pool offsets 139/171), pool_v2 marker PDA(pAMM, ["pool-v2", mint]),
  boost_vault_ata = ATA(PDA(pAMM, ["boost_vault", pool]), WSOL), trader_base_ata if missing (created by the keeper
  top-level; see §7.1), graduated_at = now (first observation, conservative; O13), L_ema = L_slow = √(X·Y) of the new pool,
  rewrite every ring entry's l, llo, lhi and cached (x, y) to it (A72; ECON §5.2, §10.1),
  clear the current observation bucket, emit VenueStateChanged.
```

**Precedence (D1).** A pump coin with a live curve or a canonical PumpSwap pool always uses those venues, even if a deeper Raydium pool exists; `create_market` and `propose_pool` reject Raydium candidates for such markets (`PumpVenuePrecedence`; on a list-only market `propose_pool` runs `classify_pump` on the stored detection set, and `execute_pool_switch` cancels a window once it shows a pump venue). A `None → pump venue` transition must also clear a collection window (`fp_*`): carried over to M5, where `crank_price` gains that transition (until then such a window can only be cancelled). Whether a deeper Raydium pool should ever win is part of owner item O18 (§10); the build keeps D1 precedence.

**Raydium-graduated pump coins and other Raydium tokens.** These map to `RaydiumV4` / `RaydiumCpmm`, not to `None`. The frontend and the keeper run `eligible()` off-chain (same `shortwin-venue-state` code) and always pass the eligible candidate with the highest `D` as `venue_hint`; `Auto` is for tokens with no eligible pool. A market created as `None` anyway (by anyone) is moved onto its pool by the permissionless `propose_pool`. Example: Fartcoin's curve is complete with zeroed reserves and has no canonical PumpSwap pool [V3], so its market adopts AMM v4 pool `Bzc9NZfM…` (38,100 SOL real, λ 99.78 %) [R-RAY §6.4].

**Is the curve complete and not yet migrated?** Test `complete && virtual_token_reserves > 0`. Just before migration a completed curve still has `vT = 279.9e12`; after migration every reserve is 0 [R-PUMP §2.1, Corrections 1]. Pump coins that graduated to Raydium before PumpSwap show exactly the zeroed state with no canonical pool [V3], and now map to a Raydium venue through the hint or `propose_pool`.

### 2.6 Raydium pool selection (A42 as amended by A60, A71; decision 7)

A mint can have many Raydium SOL pools on both programs; anyone can create one for 0.15 SOL, and neither program derives the pool from the mint [R-RAY §2.1, §2.4, §6.1]. The market must not adopt a thin pool (cheap manipulation), a pool whose liquidity can be pulled, or misread which side is SOL. v0.4 also makes the rule simpler: **a market with open positions changes its pool only when the incumbent fails.**

**Verification of a candidate** (every check on-chain; every account mandatory and key-checked) [R-RAY §6.2]:

- **AMM v4 pool `P`:** `P.owner == 675kPX9…`, `data_len == 752`; `status ∈ {1, 6, 7}`; `nonce == 254` (so the authority is the constant `5Q544f…`); `{coin_vault_mint, pc_vault_mint} == {WSOL, market.mint}` recording which side is SOL (WSOL must be exactly `So111…112`); the passed vaults equal `P.coin_vault` / `P.pc_vault`, are Tokenkeg accounts with the right mints and authority `5Q544f…`; `market.token_program == Tokenkeg` (AMM v4 is SPL-Token only).
- **CPMM pool `P`:** `P.owner == CPMMoo8…`, `data_len == 637`, discriminator `f7ede3f5d7c3de46`; `{token_0_mint, token_1_mint} == {WSOL, market.mint}` recording the SOL side; the WSOL side's token program is Tokenkeg (the Token-2022 native mint is rejected), the token side's program equals the mint owner; the vaults equal `P.token_{0,1}_vault` with the right mints and authority `GpMZb…`; `amm_config == P.amm_config` (owner CPMM, discriminator `daf42168cbcb2b6f`); `observation == P.observation_key`.
- **Both:** the LP mint equals `P.lp_mint` (Tokenkeg; `supply` at offset 36).

**Eligibility** (`eligible(P)`; as built since the M4 security review, M4-SEC-1 and M4-SEC-3): verification passes; `venue_executable(P)` (§5.3) and one-way fee `φ_ppm ≤ venue_fee_cap_bps·100` (§4.2), else `PoolIneligible`; and the **locked-depth floor**, else `PoolTooThin`:

```
⌊⌊D·isqrt(P_ref)/10⁹⌋·10⁴/λ_min_bps⌋ ≥ pool_min_real_sol
P_ref = the market's P_ema when it has a price (ring_count > 0, P_ema > 0), else the candidate's own spot
        (a Raydium listing; a list-only market that never had a pool)
```

It is the pool's locked SOL at `P_ref` over `λ_min`, exactly the `D` term of rule 5's `R_eff` (§4.7). `D` moves with neither unlocked deposits and withdrawals nor swaps (but for the fee the pool keeps), and on a market with a price neither does `P_ref`, so neither one transaction nor an unlocked deposit that stays in the pool changes eligibility. The floor keeps the lasting guarantee of v0.4.1's pair `R ≥ pool_min_real_sol ∧ λ ≥ λ_min` — at least `λ_min` of the floor stays locked whatever the unlocked LPs do — and admits every pool that pair admitted at the same price (property-tested), plus fully locked pools of 12.5–25 SOL and pools with `λ < λ_min` that hold enough locked SOL; opens stay gated by the price warm-up and `R_eff` (rules 1, 5), and a collection window takes neither under 25 SOL of locked SOL (the window floor below). **v0.4.1's clauses on the live reserve `R ≥ pool_min_real_sol` and on `λ_now ≥ pool_lock_min_bps` are gone:** an unlocked deposit as large as a pool kept it ineligible for as long as the deposit stayed (no adoption, no fast path — a halted market's underwater positions stayed frozen at the pre-halt `P_ema` until S3 at their deadline; M4-SEC-1), and `[buy | withdraw LP, propose_pool / execute_pool_switch, sell | re-deposit]` passed a pool under the floor, or failed one above it, for one instruction (M4-SEC-3). `PoolLockTooLow` (6048) is kept but no longer returned. `create_market`, `propose_pool` and `execute_pool_switch` all apply this rule (the keeper and the frontend run the same `pool_select::eligible`).

**The window floor** (as built since the M4 verification, M4V-1; owner item O18). A collection window's candidate — whose adoption moves **open** positions: a Raydium market's fast path, a list-only market with open positions — must also hold its locked SOL at `P_ref` itself at the SOL floor, `⌊D·isqrt(P_ref)/10⁹⌋ ≥ pool_min_real_sol` (no `/λ_min`; `pool_select::position_floor_ok`, `eligible_with_positions`), when a proposal opens or improves the window (else `PoolTooThin`) and again at the execution (else the window is cancelled). **Where it is judged (M4R-2; PROPOSED, row 46):** a proposal that opens or improves a window judges it at `p_win = min(P_ema, P_cand)`, `P_cand = adoption.state.price()` (the candidate's pre-trade `⌊Y·WAD/X⌋` in this instruction; `P_cand` alone on a market without a price); the execution keeps `P_ema`, with no deferral. `P_ema` does not move while the incumbent cannot execute, so a floor at `P_ema` alone admitted, after a dump during the halt, a pool with only `floor·√(P_live/P_ema)` of real locked SOL — 50 % of the floor after a 4× dump, 25 % after 16× — not "slightly under a floor", as M4's residual (3) said while ECON §6.2 claimed open positions move only onto pools at the floor (the M4R re-verification's M4R-2; both documents now state the rule and its residual 4 alike). A proposer's swap moves only its own `P_cand`, so a dump wrap can only hurt its own proposal; `eligible()`'s `/λ_min` floor stays at `P_ema`. The listing floor admits fully locked pools of only `λ_min·pool_min_real_sol` (12.5 SOL): harmless where no position is carried — listings, list-only and zero-position adoptions keep it, and their opens stay gated (rules 1, 5) — but the fast path moved open positions onto 13- and 20-SOL pools without any manipulation, the harm M4-SEC-3 (b) named (M4V-1; `m4v_it::m4v_1_*`: both refused, a 26-SOL pool adopted). Like the listing floor it reads only `D` and prices, so no LP round trip moves it, and the only price a swap moves is the proposal's own `P_cand`, which can only lower `p_win` (`m4_sec_it::m4_sec_3_*` b: a 20-SOL candidate refused plainly and inside `[buy, propose, sell]`); the residual is a pump-wrapped window-opening proposal, which restores M4's judgment at `P_ema` (residual 4 below). The keeper proposes a window only with a candidate that passes it.

`λ` and `D`:

```
λ_bps = ⌊(lp_counter − lp_mint.supply)·10⁴/lp_counter⌋      AMM v4: lp_counter = AmmInfo.lp_amount @720; CPMM: PoolState.lp_supply @333
```

The internal counters change only inside the venue program, and LP burned outside it stays in the counter, so `counter − circulating supply` is LP that can never be redeemed [R-RAY §6.3]. LP held in lockers counts as circulating, which is conservative. The main pools of all seven D2 target coins have λ between 92 % and 99.8 % [R-RAY §6.4].

**Coverage gap (V2-11; owner item O18).** Launchpads that *lock* LP in a locker program instead of burning it produce pools with λ ≈ 0 (`D ≈ 0`), which are never eligible, so their tokens are list-only despite D2. (The survey below counted pools under v0.4.1's `λ ≥ 50 %` clause; under the M4 floor a pool with `λ < 50 %` is eligible once its locked SOL reaches `λ_min·pool_min_real_sol`, 12.5 SOL — 25 SOL as a collection window's candidate, the window floor (M4V-1); the λ ≈ 0 examples stay list-only.) Live survey on 2026-09-27 [V16]: 1,668 CPMM pools with the creator fee enabled and WSOL as token 0; 311 of them hold ≥ 25 SOL of real reserve, and 67 of those have λ < 50 % — e.g. `AtqUb7Dh…` (2,024.6 SOL, λ 0.0000), `CheJUxVQ…` (608.6 SOL, λ 0.0000), `7H59ffyZ…` (465.5 SOL, λ 0.0000) — while letsbonk-style pools that burn LP pass (`FQd8z3…` 547.2 SOL, λ 0.998; `koZZXrKJ…` λ 1.000). A v1.1 option is to count LP held by the vaults of specific permanent-locker programs as locked, with each program key-checked and its irreversibility proven from source.

**Metric: locked constant-product depth** `D = ⌊λ_bps·isqrt(X·Y)/10⁴⌋` on the pricing reserves (§4.1; ECON §2.1). PROPOSED refinement of [R-RAY §6.3], which used locked SOL `R·λ`:

- A single swap leaves `√(X·Y)` unchanged except for the fee the pool keeps, so an atomic `[buy → propose → sell]` cannot inflate `D` beyond that fee growth. `R·λ` would move with the price.
- **`D` can be pushed only at fee cost** (V2-7). AMM v4 keeps the whole trade fee in the vault until a later `withdrawpnl` [V14; R-RAY §2.3], CPMM keeps the LP part (≈ 84 %) [R-RAY §2.5], so volume grows `√(X·Y)`: a trade of `A` lamports on fee `f` grows it by ≈ `f·A/(2Y)`, and raising `D` by a factor `(1 + x)` through wash trading costs ≈ `2·Y·ln(1 + x)` in fees (+25 %: ≈ 0.45·Y; measured on the real AMM v4 ELF at 1.12× this first-order estimate, `pool_attacks_it::hysteresis`). The locked share `λ` of those fees stays with burned LP, so an attacker holding the unlocked LP recovers at most `(1 − λ)` of them (v0.4.1 had `λ ≥ λ_min` for every eligible pool; since M4-SEC-1 an eligible pool may have a lower `λ` if its locked SOL clears the floor). Hence the margins below.
- Adding or removing unlocked liquidity changes `√(X·Y)` and `λ` inversely, leaving `D` unchanged. The other way to raise `D` is to burn LP, which permanently donates liquidity to the pool: an honest outcome.
- Two pools of the same pair compare correctly on `√(X·Y)`: it equals the SOL reserve divided by `√P`, so it ranks SOL depth at a common price.

**Rule.**

1. **Listing.** `create_market(venue_hint = RaydiumX)` adopts an eligible candidate (eligible at its own spot: a new market has no `P_ema`); `pool_seen_at = now`. A list-only (`None`) market with no open position adopts any eligible candidate through `propose_pool` in the same way (kind listing; D1 checked on its detection set, §2.5; a market that never had a price starts the pool's price state, as a listing does). Opens stay disabled until `pool_seen_at + pool_list_window_s` (1 h) **and until the observation state is confirmed, `ema_warm == 0`** (`EmaWarmingUp`; **PROPOSED**, M4V-5 as unified by M4R, row 46): a Raydium listing or a list-only market's first adoption starts the price state with `Observation::new_raydium` (`L = min(√(X·Y), cap)`) and marks it (`ema_warm = EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS` = `RING_LEN` = 8), so the first open waits for 8 integrations since the start, every one of a bucket recorded after it; since M4R every Raydium → Raydium adoption marks it too (rule 4). A pushed spot seeds such a price state at no cost (residual 1 below), and without honest observations `R_eff` and the spot/EMA check read it back; the keeper therefore observes zero-position Raydium markets through and at the end of their listing window (§7.1 duty 1). The warm-up integrations re-seed `P_ema` to the lowest post-start bucket low (§4.4), so a start price pushed up is gone as soon as one warm-up bucket holds an honest observation (before M4R, 8 honest per-second buckets moved `P_ema` only `1 − (5/6)⁸ ≈ 77 %` of the way); one pushed down recovers by at most the largest v0.4.1 step per integration (M4X-1; the safe side: opens stay refused on the spot/EMA check and `R_eff` meanwhile); the window's observations every 60 s continue it. During that window `propose_pool` adopts a candidate immediately if the market has **no open position** and `D_cand ≥ (1 + pool_list_margin_bps/10⁴)·D_inc` (5 %: `D` can be pushed only at fee cost, V2-7). The zero-position condition is the build's reading of decision 7 and I15: a listing window can follow a fast path taken with positions open, and those positions must not see a second pool change that no failure caused. After a thin listing is replaced, opens right after the window can still fail `PoolTooThin` until `L_slow` (τ = 1 h) converges, because an adoption never raises `L_slow` (rule 4; measured by the keeper and engine tracks: conservative by design, ops and the UI should expect it).
2. **Adoption without a failure — only while `open_positions == 0`** (decision 7). After the window, `propose_pool(candidate)` adopts the candidate immediately if the market has no open position and `D_cand ≥ (1 + h)·D_inc` with `h = pool_switch_margin_bps` (25 %); otherwise it fails with `PositionsOpen` or `PoolNotDominant`. As built, such a candidate is adopted at once even while the incumbent fails or a collection window runs (the adoption clears the window). The incumbent's own pool is never a candidate (`PoolNotDominant`). There is no pending switch, no delay and no `execute_pool_switch` for this path. Open positions therefore never see a pool change that they could not also see on a failure, and the v0.3 four-timer state (window, margin, pending switch with delay, fast-path window) shrinks to two.
   **Griefing path (disclosed; R4B-10).** One minimum-size position (0.1 SOL notional; Tier A up to 30 days, re-opened at expiry) keeps `open_positions ≥ 1` and so pins the market to an eligible but much thinner incumbent — lower caps and tier, cheaper manipulation — until that incumbent fails. No lender funds are at risk; it is a quality regression from v0.3's pending switch. Remedy without new state: Emergency pauses opens on the market (existing power, SPEC §9), the market drains within the longest open duration, and the keeper's duty 12 adopts the dominant pool at `open_positions == 0`. A delayed adoption with positions open (e.g. `D_cand ≥ 2·D_inc` for 1 h) was considered and not adopted: it re-creates the pending switch that decision 7 removed. Owner item O18 lists it.
3. **Fast path** (incumbent failure; A71; V1-7, V2-1, V2-7, R3A-4, R3B-8). The trigger is its own predicate and its own chain:
   ```
   pool_fail := !venue_executable(incumbent) ∨ φ_live(incumbent) > config.venue_fee_hard_cap_bps     (venue-side evidence only)
   chain (prologue step 8, Raydium markets): on a pool_fail observation, if pool_fail_since == 0 or
       now − last_pool_fail_obs > S3_OBS_GAP_S then pool_fail_since = now; always last_pool_fail_obs = now;
       an observation without pool_fail clears both
   ```
   The Emergency route-broken flag is **not** part of `pool_fail` (T-S3-BROKEN asserts the chain is identical with and without the flag), and the chain is not the S3 chain (which is fed by `s3_no_route`). Once `now − pool_fail_since ≥ pool_fast_route_s` (120 s) and this instruction's own observation fails `pool_fail` (the **armed** fast path), the first `propose_pool` with `D_cand ≥ pool_fast_min_bps·pool_d/10⁴` (`pool_d` = the incumbent's locked depth at its last executable observation) and the window floor at `min(P_ema, P_cand)` (M4V-1, M4R-2) opens a **collection window** of `pool_fast_window_s` (60 s) at `fp_t0 = now`, recording the candidate's keys, `D` and kind (`fp_keys`, `fp_d`, `fp_venue`; M4 also recorded `λ` in `fp_lambda_bps`, retired by M4R; event `PoolProposed`). During the window an eligible candidate with a strictly higher `D` (and still `≥ pool_fast_min_bps` of `pool_d`, and the window floor at `min(P_ema, P_cand)`) replaces the one in `fp_*`, keeping `fp_t0`; the window's own pool cannot re-propose itself (`PoolNotDominant`: an improvement must be another, strictly deeper pool). `execute_pool_switch` after the window re-verifies the recorded pool in full (the same keys, eligible and above the window floor now at `P_ema`) and adopts it only if the incumbent still fails `pool_fail` **and the chain that armed the window is unbroken**: `pool_fail_since ≠ 0` and `pool_fail_since + pool_fast_route_s ≤ fp_t0` (M4-SEC-2 — an incumbent that recovered after the window opened and failed again, or a stale window followed hours later by a one-second failure, must not move positions); any other outcome cancels and clears the window (event `PoolSwitchCancelled`), never a revert; it does not re-check `pool_fast_min_bps`. On a Raydium market improvements need the same unbroken chain; a proposal while the window has run out or its chain is broken fails `PoolWindowClosed` (6100) until `execute_pool_switch` resolves the window. Every proposal waits while **the candidate's own** Raydium program is flagged in `VenueGuard` (`VenueUpgraded`; M4-SEC-8, §2.3.2), while an upgrade of the other Raydium program — typically the failing incumbent's — holds nothing up. An execution whose window candidate's program is flagged treats it as **not adoptable now and cancels the window** (as built since the M4 verification, M4V-4): M4-SEC-8's execution reverted and kept the window, and with it `PoolWindowClosed` for every other proposal — the unflagged program's included — until the Emergency review, freezing the fast path while the halted incumbent's positions ran towards S3. The `pool_fail` chain stays armed, so a proposal on the other program opens a new window at once (`m4v_it::m4v_4_*`: adopted after 240 s of `pool_fail`, S3 wait 600 s), and after `revalidate_venue` the flagged program's pool can be proposed again. The fast path works whether or not positions are open. Its whole duration fits inside the shortest S3 window any position can have (`pool_fast_route_s + pool_fast_window_s ≤ 540 = 600 − 60`, compiled, R3A-11 b). A **list-only market with open positions** (reachable from M5, §2.5) uses the same window without arming or chain (M4-SEC-4): its first eligible proposal opens it, a strictly deeper one improves it, and the execution adopts the deepest candidate seen as kind fast, instead of pinning the positions to whichever eligible pool is proposed first.
4. **Every adoption** — listing, listing-window replacement, zero-position adoption, fast path — may change the venue kind between `RaydiumV4` and `RaydiumCpmm`, and:
   - the pool itself is written whole: venue, pool cache (every other slot cleared, so no CPMM config or observation of a previous pool stays), SOL side, `pool_d = D`;
   - `P_ema` carries over (same asset, arbitraged) until the warm-up re-seeds it (below); a list-only market that never had a price instead starts the pool's price state, as a listing does;
   - **the adoption reads no sample of the new pool** (PROPOSED, M4R, row 46; ECON §5.2; `engine::pool_select::adopt(market, a, now, list_window_s, lambda_min_bps)`, no `l_rec`): with `D` its locked depth and `cap = pool_select::depth_at_lambda(D, λ_min) = ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋`,
     ```
     a priced market:    observe::rewrite_liquidity(cap, RaydiumLocked { slow_cap: D })   // L_ema = cap, L_slow = min(L_slow, D);
                                                                                      // every ring entry's l = llo = lhi = cap, (x, y) recomputed
                         observe::mark_restart(&mut ema)                               // ema_warm = EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS (8), ema_guard = 1
     an unpriced market: Observation::new_raydium(now, P_spot, √(X·Y), cap)            // a list-only market's first adoption: L = min(√(X·Y), cap),
                                                                                      // P_ema = P_spot, marked the same way
     ```
     `P_ema` and the ring prices carry over (same asset, arbitraged; stale after a halt), but only until the warm-up replaces them: the next 8 integrations re-seed `P_ema = min lo`, rising by at most the largest v0.4.1 step per integration (M4X-1, row 47), and `L_ema = min(max lhi, ⌊lomed(lhi)·(10⁴ + C)/10⁴⌋)` over the post-change entries (§4.4), and while `ema_warm > 0` on an executable venue `liquidate` / `expire` fail `EmaWarmingUp`, `sol_settle` evaluates nothing and Raydium opens wait (§3.4, §3.6–§3.8); while `ema_guard = 1` the exit reference reads `min(L_ema, L_mlo)` (§3.6) and `L_slow` integrates `min(lobs, L_mlo)` (§4.4; M4X-2). No `L_live` or `L_rec` is read: `recorded_liquidity` is deleted and `record_window` stops writing `λ`. **Why** (M4R-1): M4 as built wrote `min(L_rec, √(X·Y)_now, cap)`, the smaller of two samples taken in two transactions (M4V-2; M4-SEC-5 took the record alone, v0.4.1 the adopting instruction's own sample). A deposit could not push that up, but an unlocked LP's withdrawal around either sampling instruction wrote `D`, and after the adoption the LP holds `L_ema` there with a withdraw-wrapped crank every second (directional aggregation: one observation on its own side freezes the EMA), so a position within ≈ `q/x` of `cr_liq`, healthy on the pool as it trades, became liquidatable on the EMA leg and a withdraw-wrapped spot leg — `tests/litesvm/tests/m4r_it.rs::m4r_explore_liquidation_grid` (`M4R_GRID=19700,19750 M4R_SECS=90`): liquidated 24–26 s after the adoption when wrapped, never in 90 s when not. The carried, stale `P_ema` was a second forced path (after a mild dip during the halt an honest adoption let the LP holder liquidate a healthy position at +2 s, ECON §12.12 (9)), and on a `λ < λ_min` pool M4's honest cap `D/λ_min` was already the start of such a band (ECON §12.12 (2)). The anchor reads nothing any party can wrap; the seed reads bucket **highs** for `L` and **lows** for `P`, the sides a wrapped sample cannot use to force an exit, so an honest warm-up ends with `L_ema` equal to the pool's depth for every λ (λ 10–99.78 %: 1.000 at the gate) and the box narrow from the gate on (M4V-3 and M4-SEC-6 resolved), and forcing needs all 8 warm-up buckets attacker-only (residual 1);
   - **every ring entry's `l`, `llo` and `lhi` are rewritten to the anchor and its `(x, y)` recomputed** (A72; R3B-7 a, R3A-11 d): with carry-over, the ring median kept the old pool's liquidity and the settlement box stayed more than 1 % wide in `L` for 3–4 s after each change (ECON §10.1; the fork replay of `venue_switch.py` measured 5 s without the rewrite and 0 s with it, `pool_attacks_it`). Since M4R the ring is read during the warm-up only where nothing is gated — a new pool that cannot execute (S3 reads the partly anchored ring, residual 8) — and the warm-up replaces every entry before the gate opens (`venue_switch.py` (a): 4 wide seconds with or without the rewrite, all inside the gate);
   - the current observation bucket is cleared; both route chains and the collection window (`fp_*`) are cleared (the new pool is observed afresh; the tier is recomputed by the next crank or open);
   - `graduated_at` is kept (age is the token's time on an eligible pool); open positions keep all locked terms and exit on the new pool;
   - `pool_seen_at = now` **only if the market is not already inside a listing window**, so opens wait `pool_list_window_s` after an adoption outside a window while the `D` ranking settles, and a chain of proposals cannot extend a window;
   - a list-only market takes the candidate's age start as `graduated_at` (O13, O13b) and, for a curve that graduated elsewhere, the `graduated_elsewhere` flag;
   - emit `PoolSwitched` (one event per instruction: neither pool-selection instruction emits `VenueStateChanged` or `RouteChainChanged`).
5. **Opens are sized on locked depth, not refused on `λ_now`** (V2-8). Every liquidity input of an open is bounded by the locked depth, which unlocked deposits cannot move: tier liquidity uses `R_eff = min(R_now, R_slow, ⌊⌊D·isqrt(P_ema)/10⁹⌋·10⁴/λ_min_bps⌋)` (§4.7), and the conservative token reserve for caps is `min(X_now, ⌊L_slow·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋, ⌊D·10⁹·10⁴/(λ_min_bps·isqrt⁺(P_ema))⌋)` (§4.6; ECON §2.1). Opens require `R_eff ≥ pool_min_real_sol`. Withdrawing *all* unlocked LP leaves at least `λ_min` (50 %) of the depth a position was sized on. Since M4-SEC-1 eligibility and adoption do not read `λ_now` either: they test the same locked-depth term at `P_ema` (the floor above), so `λ_min` only scales that floor and these sizing bounds (v0.4.1: "`λ ≥ λ_min` is still required for eligibility and adoption").

| Attack | Outcome under the rule |
|---|---|
| Fake deep pool with self-supplied liquidity, pulled after capture | Unlocked liquidity adds nothing to `D`; winning requires burning LP worth `(1 + h)` times the incumbent's locked depth, which permanently deepens the market, and it can happen only while no position is open |
| Price push on the candidate at proposal time (atomic bundle) | `√(X·Y)` is invariant under swaps; no effect |
| Thin pools used for price manipulation | Never read. The market prices, observes and executes only on the adopted pool, whose keys are stored and re-checked on every instruction |
| Mint on the wrong side, fake quote mint | The SOL side is derived from the verified mint fields; the quote must be exactly `So111…112` under Tokenkeg |
| Proposal spam | A weaker proposal cannot displace the incumbent or a better fast-path candidate; each fake pool costs 0.15 SOL [R-RAY §6.3]; adoptions inside a listing window do not extend it |
| Wash trading to raise `D` (V2-7) | Costs ≈ `2·Y·ln(1 + x)` in fees (1.12× on the real AMM v4 ELF), of which the locked share `λ` stays with burned LP (`≥ λ_min` for pools v0.4.1 admitted); the 5 % listing margin and the 25 % adoption margin make flips expensive; with positions open only a failure can change the pool |
| Front-running the fast path with an own pool carrying unlocked LP (V2-7) | The collection window adopts the deepest eligible candidate seen; an own pool wins only if it is the deepest, and its locked depth `D` — the most positions are sized on is `D/λ_min`, rule 5 — cannot be withdrawn afterwards (`pool_attacks_it::front_run`: after the pull, `D`, `pool_d` and the sizing are unchanged) |
| Emergency flag used to arm the fast path (V1-7) | Impossible: `pool_fail` does not read the flag (T-S3-BROKEN) |
| Adding unlocked liquidity to block opens (V2-8) | No effect: opens do not check `λ_now`; sizing uses locked depth |
| Adding unlocked liquidity to every alternate pool, and leaving it there, to keep them ineligible: no adoption, no fast path, a halted market's positions frozen until S3 at the pre-halt `P_ema` (M4-SEC-1) | No effect since M4: eligibility is the locked-depth floor at `P_ema`, which a deposit does not move (`m4_sec_it::m4_sec_1_*`: with and without the deposit the halted market's window opens at 120 s, the candidate is adopted after it and the underwater position is liquidated there) |
| Atomic swap or LP round trip around `propose_pool` / `execute_pool_switch` (`[buy / withdraw LP, act, sell / re-deposit]`: a pool under the floor adopted, or a window cancelled; M4-SEC-3 and the implementer's two tripwires) | No effect on a market with a price: `D` and `P_ema` do not move inside a transaction (the tripwires are inverted: `pool_attacks_it::lambda_games::lp_dilution_no_longer_cancels_the_fast_path` and `…::a_round_trip_under_the_sol_floor_no_longer_cancels_the_fast_path`). Residual (disclosed): a list-only market that never had a price judges the floor at the candidate's spot, so `[buy, propose_pool, sell]` can adopt a pool under it; bounded: such a market has no position, its first open waits for the price warm-up (`EmaWarmingUp`, M4V-5) and the keeper's listing-window observations, after which `R_eff` refuses it (`PoolTooThin`, rule 5; `m4v_it::m4v_5_*`), and a zero-position adoption or the listing window replaces the pool |
| A recovered incumbent that fails again, or a stale window followed by a one-second failure (M4-SEC-2) | The window adopts only while the chain that armed it holds; otherwise it cancels, and a new failure re-arms after `pool_fast_route_s` (`m4_sec_it::m4_sec_2_*`) |
| LP moves of the winning candidate around the adopting instruction, to write its locked-only depth (or an inflated one) into the ring (M4-SEC-5) | No effect since M4R: the adoption reads no sample (rule 4); `L_ema` is the anchor `D/λ_min` until the warm-up seeds it from the new pool's bucket highs (`m4_sec_it::m4_sec_5_*`: a withdrawal around the execution changes nothing). M4 as built wrote `min(L_rec, L_live, D/λ_min)`, which a withdrawal around either sample pushed to `D` — a forcing path, not a delay (next row) |
| An unlocked LP of the candidate withdraws around a sampling instruction, then holds `L_ema` at `D` with a withdraw-wrapped crank and tries a withdraw-wrapped liquidation every second (M4R-1) | No forced exit (M4R, PROPOSED): nothing is sampled, the seed never reads a withdraw-wrapped low, and exits wait for the gate (ECON §12.12 (1): M4 as built liquidated every wrapped row from 1.97–1.98× up to the 2.01× threshold, 21–41 s after the adoption; M4R none, and genuine liquidations at +9 s; 1,200 targeted random runs: 0 forced, as built 30 of 615; `m4r_it::m4r1_a_withdraw_wrapped_switch_forces_no_liquidation`). On a `λ < λ_min` candidate as built forced 2.00–2.03×, M4R nothing. Residual 1: a warm-up whose 8 buckets are all attacker-only |
| A deposit around the recording proposal (anyone with capital, no LP needed) to inflate the ring after the fast path (M4V-2) | No effect: no sample is read (M4R; M4 as built: bounded by the execution's own sample, `m4v_it::m4v_2_*`) |
| A deposit around one crank every second after the adoption (a capital freeze: the pool's size in both assets, no fee), holding `L_ema` up so that the exit reference stays below the live cost until S1 — also held for 10–30 min, so that the frozen `L_ema` climbs into `L_slow`, which `L_b` reads unguarded (M4X-2) | The reference guard (M4R): while `ema_guard = 1`, `L_b` reads `min(L_ema, L_mlo)` (§3.6) and `L_slow` integrates `min(lobs, L_mlo)` (§4.4, M4X-2) — RA → 630 SOL liquidates at +61 s at 1.732×, lenders +0.834 SOL; without the guard S1 at +123 s, −17.785 SOL; held 900 s on the fork, `L_slow` stays at 0.998× the depth and the genuine pump is liquidated, where the guard on `L_ema` alone let `L_slow` reach 2.621× and end in S1, lenders −10.056 SOL (ECON §12.12 (4); `m4v_it::m4v_2c_a_deposit_freeze_does_not_block_the_liquidation`, `m4r_it::m4x2_a_held_deposit_freeze_no_longer_lifts_l_slow`). Residual 2: the liquidation trigger waits ≈ `(1 − H/L)·q/x` in price while the freeze is paid |
| A dump during the halt, then a window on a pool that clears the floor only at the frozen `P_ema` (M4R-2) | Refused `PoolTooThin`: a proposal judges the window floor at `min(P_ema, P_cand)` (`m4r_it::m4r2_a_dump_during_the_halt_keeps_a_thin_pool_out`). Residual 4: the pool's backer pump-wraps its own window-opening proposal (0.03–0.04 SOL) and restores M4's judgment at `P_ema`, never looser; and a genuine dump **inside** the window, after the proposal that opened it, is judged by the execution at `P_ema` only and adopted (M4X-3; `m4r_it::m4x3_a_dump_inside_the_window_is_adopted_at_p_ema_then_re_seeded`) |
| Attacker-only warm-up seconds (the keeper absent, congested or censored; the attacker may execute the switch itself and crank in its second) filled with `[buy, crank_price, sell]` bundles, then `[buy, liquidate, sell]` at the gate (M4X-1) | Bounded as in steady state: each warm-up integration raises the price seed by at most the largest v0.4.1 step (`×(τ + dt·(1 + C))/(τ + dt)`, `observe::price_up_bound`), so these seconds force exactly the thresholds the same seconds force in steady state and forced under M4 as built, never before the gate (ECON §12.12 (8), part 8d; `m4r_it::m4x1_attacker_only_price_bundles_reach_no_further_than_steady_state`: a ≈ 3× threshold, liquidated at a true 1.00× at +9 s before M4X-1, now open). Residual 1 |
| Stale-high `P_ema` carried over after a mild dip during the halt, with the LP holder's wrapped cranks and liquidations | Open (M4R): the warm-up seeds a fresh `P_ema` (ECON §12.12 (9): as built liquidated at +2 s a position whose CR at the pool's depth was 1.508–1.516) |
| An unlocked-LP holder's withdraw contest while the guard is set, then a withdraw-wrapped liquidation of a genuinely liquidatable position | An extraction, not a forced exit: the reference falls to `max(D, L_slow)`, and the borrower overpays by at most `(1/λ − 1)·q/x` of `B` (+4.04 % at λ 50 %, as M4 as built allowed through its `D` seed; residual 3) |
| An honest, fully locked pool of 12.5–25 SOL adopted by the fast path under open positions — no manipulation needed (M4V-1) | Refused: a window's candidate needs its locked SOL at `P_ema` alone at the SOL floor (the window floor; `m4v_it::m4v_1_*`: 20- and 13-SOL candidates refused `PoolTooThin`, a 26-SOL one adopted) |
| An upgrade flagged on the window candidate's program during the window, freezing the fast path (every other proposal `PoolWindowClosed`) until the Emergency review (M4V-4) | The execution cancels the window; the chain stays armed, and a candidate on the other program opens the next window at once (`m4v_it::m4v_4_*`) |
| Capture of a list-only market at a pushed spot, then `[buy, open_short, sell]` an hour later before anyone observed it, pinning the market to the sub-floor pool (M4V-5) | Refused `EmaWarmingUp` until `ema_warm == 0` (8 integrations after the start, which re-seed `P_ema` to the lowest post-start bucket low; M4R); the keeper observes zero-position Raydium markets through and at the end of their listing window, after which `R_eff` or the spot/EMA check refuses the pushed open (`m4v_it::m4v_5_*`; keeper `a_listing_window_is_observed_and_the_first_open_is_warm`, `an_unobserved_listing_window_is_warmed_up_after_it`). Residual (disclosed): an attacker who fills the 8 warm-up buckets with attacker-only seconds and then keeps the spot pushed in **every** observed second (an atomic `[buy, crank, sell]` in each; directional aggregation keeps a pushed `P_ema` inside such buckets) can still warm the price state up at the pushed level, paying a round trip in every second the keeper observes (every 60 s through the window, every second in its last 10 s, every slot of duty 2's bursts); lender funds stay overcollateralized at the true price (19.3× in the verification's run) — the harm remains a capture and R4B-10 pinning of a list-only token |
| Positions on a list-only market pinned to the first eligible proposal (from M5; M4-SEC-4) | They collect candidates in a window and the deepest is adopted (`m4_sec_it::m4_sec_4_*`: an attacker's 26-SOL pool opens the window, Fartcoin's main pool, ≈ 1,470× deeper, replaces it and is adopted; a pump curve appearing during the window cancels it, D1) |
| An upgrade of one Raydium program used to stop the fast path onto the other (M4-SEC-8) | Candidates latch only on their own program's bit (`m4_sec_it::m4_sec_8_*`) |
| Junk pools created for a mint (≈ 0.17 SOL each) to slow the keeper during an incident (M4-SEC-7) | The keeper ranks discovered pools by locked SOL, reads at most `rank_keys_per_tick` accounts a tick and evaluates the best `max_candidates`, after the crank batch (§7.1 duty 12; keeper scenario `junk_pools_cost_a_bounded_ranking_after_the_cranks`: 300 junk pools, adoption at the same second) |
| An attacker's own fast-path candidate held at a pushed price (no arbitrage) | Not refused: no instruction compares a candidate's spot with `P_ema`. The persistence rules still hold (atomic refusal, `liq_persist_n`, `settle_persist_n`); the attacker's only candidate (`D` ≈ 29 % of `pool_d`) held at 3× the price made the position liquidatable after 7 s and settled it S1 after 63 s (`pool_attacks_it::mid_position::an_attacker_cannot_force_an_exit_through_a_switch`; under M4R no exit before the gate at +9 s, and a push held through all 8 warm-up seconds is residual 5). Disclosed with O18 (a spot-vs-`P_ema` check or an ECON §12 held-model row for a 25 % fast-path pool are the options) |
| Unlocked LPs withdraw from the incumbent | `R_now` falls, so tier liquidity and size caps fall at once; open positions keep at least `λ_min` of the depth they were sized on (rule 5), which at capped sizes cannot reach S2 (ECON §8.6); FV follows the fast liquidity EMA (D3 literal) |

**Residuals of the M4 rules** (disclosed; owner item O18; as amended by the M4 verification and by M4R, row 46): (a) a list-only market without a price judges the floor at the candidate's spot, which one transaction can push; bounded by the price warm-up and the keeper's listing-window observations (M4V-5), with the sustained-bundle residual of the table above; (b) no fast-path candidate's spot is compared with `P_ema` beyond the window floor at `min(P_ema, P_cand)` (table above). M4 as built's residuals (2), (3) and (5) — the two-sample `[D, D/λ_min]` seed, the floors at a stale `P_ema`, the box delay after a `λ < λ_min` switch — are replaced by M4R's, all disclosed with O18 (ECON §12.12):

1. **Degraded mode.** If every one of the 8 warm-up buckets is attacker-only (keeper absent or censored for the whole warm-up; 7 keeper-absent seconds when the attacker executes the switch and cranks in its second), the attacker can seed `L_ema = D` with withdraw-wrapped cranks, and an 8-s genuine held drain does the same; the M4R-1 band then applies at +9 s (M4 as built was forced whatever the keeper did). On pools with `λ < 1/(1 + C)` (50 %), 4 attacker-only warm-up buckets suffice, because the seed falls to `(1 + C)·D < H`. Steady state needs 3 attacker-only seconds for the same band (10). On the price side, pump-wrapped cranks and a pump-wrapped liquidation in those seconds raise the seed by at most the largest v0.4.1 step per integration (M4X-1): a position within ≈ 2.8× of the carried `P_ema` can be liquidated at the gate at the attacker's pumped price — what the same attacker-only seconds force in steady state and forced under M4 as built (ECON §12.12 (8)), never earlier. Before M4X-1 the seed read the pump itself and 8 such seconds forced any threshold.
2. **Capital deposit freeze.** A deposit-wrapped warm-up crank, then deposit-wrapped cranks every second, hold `L_ema ≤ (1 + C)·H` (higher only if ≥ 5 of the 8 warm-up buckets are pushed), with about the pool's size in both assets re-deposited every second and no fee. The EMA leg is delayed by at most ≈ `(1 − H/L)·q/x` in price, and at a flat price for as long as the freeze lasts; the box stays wide, so S2/S3 amounts wait (the ECON §10.5 class). **The reference is not blocked** (the guard on the `L_ema` term and on `L_slow`'s input), however long the freeze is held (M4X-2: with the `L_ema` term alone, `L_slow` reached 1.4–2.6× the depth after 10–30 min and the reference blocked again).
3. **Withdraw contest while the guard is set.** An unlocked-LP holder who withdraw-wraps ≥ 4 of the 8 warm-up seconds and ≥ 4 of every 8 after, then withdraw-wraps a liquidation of a genuinely liquidatable position, makes the borrower overpay by at most `cost(D)/cost(H) − 1 ≈ (1/λ − 1)·q/x` of `B` (≈ 0 on λ ≈ 1 pools; +4.04 %, 1.24 SOL, in the sim, which M4 as built also allowed through its `D` seed). It never forces an exit.
4. **M4R-2 is closed only for honest dumps before the window opens.** One pump-wrapped window-opening proposal by the thin pool's backer (0.03–0.04 SOL) restores M4's `P_ema` judgment, never looser; a genuine dump inside the 60-s window, after the proposal that opened it, is judged by the execution at `P_ema` alone and adopted (M4X-3; never looser than as built; the warm-up then re-seeds `P_ema` at the live price and opens are refused `PoolTooThin`). The execution does not re-read the candidate's one-instruction spot: a failure there would either revert (every other proposal held, the M4V-4 freeze) or cancel (a dump-wrapped execution then cancels windows until S3). A tracked-EMA execution floor needs a window-observation path and was not adopted; a follow-up option is a `pool_fail` clause for an incumbent below the floor at the confirmed `P_ema`.
5. **Held push during the warm-up.** Holding the pool pumped for all 8 warm-up seconds (genuine price for every observation) seeds `P_ema` high, by at most the largest v0.4.1 step per integration: the steady-state held class (ECON §12.6; +9 s vs as built +10 s; with no keeper at all, for a held 3× push on a near-threshold position, 9 s and −0.38 SOL, as built 8 s and −0.27). Atomic bundles in attacker-only seconds are residual 1.
6. **Paid down-push of the price seed.** One dump-wrapped warm-up crank lowers the seed without bound; after the gate `P_ema` recovers at ≤ +16.7 %/s, a delay of about `ln k/ln(7/6)` s — paid, the ECON §12.9 class.
7. **Exit latency.** Adoption + 9 s, usually faster than as built because `P` is fresh (2.2×: +9 vs +13 s; 3× S1: +49 vs +61 s; λ 99.78 % at 2×: +9 vs +26 s; the big position: +9 vs +14 s); slower only where as built was already right (5× / 16× S1: +49 vs +48 s).
8. **A new pool that halts inside the warm-up.** Nothing is gated on a venue that cannot execute: positions are frozen until S3 or the next fast path, as built, and S3 reads the partly anchored ring; the price seed stays where the step bound left it (M4X-1), so after a genuine move during the halt larger than the seed reached before the new pool halted, an S3 that needs the position liquidatable waits for its deadline, as with M4 as built's carried `P_ema` (`switch_confirm_it::a_new_pool_that_halts_in_the_warm_up_is_not_gated` halts at +6 s, when the seed has reached the pool's 2.5×).
9. **Rent during the warm-up** accrues on the running-minimum `P_ema` for ≤ 8 s (lender-unfavourable, negligible).
10. **Pre-existing, not fixed here.** In steady state a genuinely reached low `L_ema` can be frozen by free withdraw-wrapped cranks: 3–5 s of held drain or attacker-only seconds, then the freeze, force the band. It needs a steady-state change (for example, the EMA leg at `max(L_i, upper median of lhi)`) and is a follow-up.
11. **Evidence.** The sims and the LiteSVM regressions on the fork (§8.2 ★T-SWITCH-CONFIRM: `m4r_it`, `switch_confirm_it`, `m4v_it`, `m4_sec_it`, with the M4R verification's M4X-1–3); CU as measured in `m3_cu` (§6.1).

---

## 3. Instructions

### 3.0 Common structure

**Remaining-accounts convention (A6; C0-2, C0-4).** Venue accounts are passed in `remaining_accounts` as

```
ReadSet(venue) ++ TradeExtras(venue) [trading instructions] ++ Extras(venue, ix) [A41]
```

and parsed by a strict per-venue parser (§3.12). v0.3's `OpenTail` (the ProgramData accounts `open_short` read for the venue guard) is gone: the guard is the `VenueGuard` latch (A7).

- **Every slot is mandatory and key-checked** against a compile-time constant, a `Market` cache entry, or an on-chain re-derivation (creator vaults). There are no optional slots and no "None marker". A missing or wrong account reverts; it never maps to "+∞", "no route" or "absent" (C0-2).
- An account that may legitimately not exist yet (the canonical PumpSwap pool before graduation, the boost-vault ATA after the boost) is still passed at its derived key; the reader reports its state ("uninitialised", amount 0). Pricing never depends on whether the caller passed it.
- **Key check versus reader failure (A63; V1-8).** The two are different events. A *wrong key* reverts, always. A *correctly keyed* venue-state account whose contents no longer parse — a changed owner, discriminator or layout of a pool, config, fee config or global account after a venue upgrade — makes the venue **not executable** (§5.3). For `crank_price` and `sol_settle` that is a normal outcome (the prologue records a no-route observation and the S3 chain runs), never a revert; trading instructions then fail at their own route check. S3 therefore stays reachable when a venue changes a state account's layout.
- **Bounded extras (A41; C0-4 (a), V2-6).** After the adapter's own list, the parser accepts exactly the trailing accounts of the extras set the caller selects in the instruction data: `none`, `current`, `previous` or `stable` (M2-SEC-2) from `VenueExtras[venue][ix]` (§2.3.2), 0–4 entries in order. As built (M2): the selection is the trailing `extras: ExtrasSel {set: u8 (0 none, 1 current, 2 previous, 3 stable), bumps: [u8; 4]}` argument of `open_short`, `close_short`, `liquidate` and `expire` (`bumps[i]` for template entry `i`; fixed entries ignore theirs), and a selected set's `Extras(venue, ix)` segment is the `VenueExtras` account itself (checked against its PDA with the stored bump, owner, discriminator, layout version and venue byte) followed by the set's entries; `none` means an empty segment. A fixed-key entry must match; a **template** entry is re-derived on-chain from its constant seeds and placeholders (`mint`, `pool`, `trader`, `bonding_curve`, `coin_creator`) with the caller's bump, which must be the **canonical** one (M2-SEC-3; §2.3.2), so per-mint and per-pool PDAs — the kind of account venues actually add (pump's `bonding_curve_v2`, PumpSwap's `pool-v2` [C1-10; R-VEN]) — can be followed. Each extra must be a non-signer, not owned by short.win and not on the denylist, and writable exactly as its flag says (as built, `BadExtras` otherwise); they are appended to the venue CPI's metas. This lets the Emergency multisig follow an *additive* venue account change (pump's 2026-04-28 upgrade added trailing accounts with 6 days' notice and allowed passing them early [R-PUMP Review corrections 4; C0-4]) without a program upgrade. Because `none` and the previous set stay selectable, one wrong or hostile Emergency write cannot brick exits even on a venue that rejects unexpected trailing accounts (pump behaviour **UNVERIFIED**, U24); because `stable` keeps a set that stood for `EXTRAS_STABLE_S` (or that Governance published), neither can several quick ones (M2-SEC-2). Raydium ignores trailing accounts [R-RAY §9.6]. A *non-additive* change cannot be followed; it reaches S3 through reader failure (above) or the route-broken flag (A40), with the liveness limits of §5.3.
- Named Anchor accounts are never repeated in remaining accounts. CPI metas are assembled from both lists, so Anchor 1.x duplicate-mutable checks never see duplicates [R-TOOL §1.3].

**Market prologue.** Every instruction that loads a `Market` for writing runs these steps in order (ECON §2.1 order of operations, §5.1–5.2, §7, §9.4):

1. Load `config` (read) and `market` (mut); check `version`.
2. **Params-version sync (A17, A59; R3A-9).**
   - EMA fields (`ema_dtmax_s`, `ema_clamp_bps`, `liq_slow_tau_s`, `liq_slow_dtmax_s`): if `market.ema_params_version ≠ config.ema_params.version` and (`open_positions == 0` or `now ≥ config.ema_params.force_at`), copy them and set `ema_params_version`.
   - Kink: if `market.kink_version ≠ config.kink.version` and `open_positions == 0`, copy `util_kink_bps` and set `kink_version`. A forced EMA adoption never touches the kink or its version, so a kink change waits for the first moment the market has no open position (test: force-adopt with positions open, drain to 0 positions, assert the kink is then adopted).
   - The ring keeps its entries (they are prices, not parameters).
3. Mint guard: `mint.key == market.mint`, `*mint.owner == market.token_program == token_program.key`. Entry paths (`create_market`, `deposit`, `open_short`) require `check_mint_safety`; exit paths (`withdraw`, `close_short`, `liquidate`, `expire`, `sol_settle`) never revert on the filter: a mint that fails it now is reported as a `MintSafetyTripped` **log** event (`emit!`, step 11) and the instruction continues [R-MINT §6, §9.4] (M1-SEC-11 / M1-FID-10).
4. **Accrue** (ECON §7, §9.4): (a) **vest release**: `rel = ⌊vest_bal·(now − vest_last)/(vest_end − vest_last)⌋` (all of it once `now ≥ vest_end`) is credited to entitlement and realised allocation together (`acc`, `acc_r`), `vest_last = now`; (b) **rent**: the three basis indices with `P̃ = min(P_ema, P_ACC_MAX)` taken **before** this instruction's roll and `U` from the pre-instruction counters; the lender credit `⌊Σ_j A_j·δJ_j/(10⁴·10⁴·31,536,000·WAD)⌋` goes to `acc` and `receivable` (§4.5).
5. **Observation roll**: if a bucket is open and `now > b_sec`: if `open_positions > 0 ∧ now − ema_ts > COVERAGE_GAP_S` emit the `CoverageGap{gap_s = now − ema_ts, open_positions}` log event (A69); integrate the bucket into `P_ema`, `L_ema`, `L_slow` and push a ring entry `(ts, p, l, x, y, lo, hi, llo, lhi)` (v0.4.1, R4B-2; §4.4): `(p, l) = (P_ema, L_ema)`, the cached `(x, y) = reprice(L_ema, P_ema)` (on the curve these are the virtual reserves `(vT_i, vQ_i)`, and `curve_reprice` applies the curve's constant offsets only when quoting, so `integrate` takes no venue argument), and `(lo, hi, llo, lhi)` the completed bucket's price and liquidity extremes (the clamped `obs`, `lobs` drive the EMA step but are not stored); move the bucket to `lb_*`; clear it (ECON §5.2).
6. If the instruction carries a ReadSet: parse it, read `VenueState`, run `sync_venue` (§2.5). Reader failures of correctly keyed accounts yield "not executable" (A63). A venue change (graduation) resets the liquidity EMAs and rewrites the ring (A72).
7. **Observation record**: if the venue is `venue_executable` (or `PumpMigrating`, whose completed curve is final) and its price is defined, add `(P_spot, √(X·Y))` of the **pre-trade** state to the bucket of second `now` (ECON §5.1). Every instruction records; there is no one-per-slot limit (C0-5 (c)). A venue that cannot execute is not observed (A64; V2-5). A fee above the hard cap does not stop observation: the pool still trades.
8. **Route chains** (§5.3): update the S3 chain with `s3_no_route`; on Raydium markets also update the fast-path chain with `pool_fail` (§2.6). Both use the **global** `venue_fee_hard_cap_bps` (R3A-4 c).
9. Instruction body. Decisions read `P_ema`, the ring and `lb_*`, which never contain this instruction's own observation (the one-second lag, ECON §5.2).
10. Post-conditions: trader state (§2.4 rule 5), `pool_vault.amount ≥ T`, vault lamport invariants (§2.3.1).
11. One `emit_cpi!` event per instruction (merging what v0.1 emitted separately), to save instruction-trace entries. `CoverageGap` and an exit path's `MintSafetyTripped` (step 3) are the two exceptions: `emit!` log events (no CPI, no trace entry), because they are advisory and can coincide with the instruction's own event; indexers also derive coverage from `PriceCranked` timestamps. `flag_unsafe_mint` emits `MintSafetyTripped` as its one `emit_cpi!` event.

**A16: lender instructions.** `deposit` and `withdraw` run steps 1–5 and 10–11: they accrue (including the vest release) and roll the bucket, but carry no venue accounts and record no observation. `claim_sol`, `cover_deficit`, `sync_lender` and `add_margin` do not write `Market` at all (C0-17); the first three write `MarketVault` (pot, lock state) and the lender. The EMA is fed by trading instructions, `sol_settle`, `propose_pool` and `crank_price`; the in-house keeper supplies one observation per second near thresholds and counts its own `sol_settle` / `liquidate` / `expire` calls as that second's observation (ECON §5.8).

### 3.1 Initialization and admin

| Instruction | Signer | Accounts | Effect | Checks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `initialize_config(args)` | short.win **upgrade authority** | `config` (init), `venue_guard` (init), `venue_extras` ×4 (init, empty), `oracle_cache` (init), `program_data` (short.win's own; `upgrade_authority == signer`), system | Writes authorities, params, tiers, EMA and kink params, `timelock_s`; `venue_guard.guard_slot` from the args | Prevents front-run initialisation. `MIN_TIMELOCK_S ≤ timelock_s ≤ MAX_TIMELOCK_S` (`TimelockTooShort` / `TimelockTooLong`). `validate_params()` (`params::validate`, then `params::validate_econ`; §2.3.3). Governance, Emergency and the fee recipient non-default; the fee recipient is taken by key only (the transaction is 1,198 of 1,232 bytes), so the deployer's choice is corrected, if needed, by an `Authorities` change |
| `propose_config(change)` | `governance` | `config` (mut) | `pending = { change, not_before = now + timelock_s }`; replaces any pending change | `validate_params()` (`params::validate`, then `params::validate_econ`; §2.3.3) on the would-be result. Off-chain gate (A73): the proposal's overrides pass `sim/config_check.py` (`OK`) before the multisig signs |
| `apply_config()` | anyone | `config` (mut); optional `fee_recipient` (mut; `Authorities` only) and `oracle_cache` (mut; `OracleFeed` only) — the program id stands for "none", and a variant that needs its account fails without it (`AccountMismatch`) | Applies `pending`, clears it. `OracleFeed` also empties `oracle_cache` (price, conf, expo, publish time, slot; the bump stays), so the old feed's price is neither served as fresh nor gates the new feed's first crank through the step or ordering rule; until the next `crank_sol_usd` tiers fail safe to C (§4.7; M1-SEC-10) | `not_before ≤ now ≤ not_before + CONFIG_APPLY_WINDOW_S` (14 d; later `PendingChangeExpired` and governance proposes again, so a forgotten proposal cannot be applied at a moment that suits whoever finds it; M1-SEC-9); the change is re-checked against the current config. **Fee-recipient rule** (`Authorities`; M1-SEC-5): the passed account equals the new `fee_recipient`, is **System-owned** and **not executable** (`InvalidParams`), and is writable, so the runtime refuses sysvars, program ids and reserved keys; a System account that does not exist yet is accepted. Variants: `Params(Params, [TierParams;4])`, `EmaParams{…}` (new `version`, `force_at`), `Kink{util_kink_bps}` (new kink `version`), `Authorities{governance, pause_authority, coverage_authority, fee_recipient}`, `Timelock(u32)` (MIN ≤ · ≤ MAX), `EnableVenues(u32)`, `OracleFeed{account, feed_id}`. **There is no variant that changes a venue program id or a venue PDA (C0-8)** |
| `cancel_config()` | `governance` (any change) or `pause_authority` (not an `Authorities` or `Timelock` change: `EmergencyCannotCancel`, owner decision on M1-SEC-1, SPEC §11) | `config` (mut) | Clears `pending` | |
| `set_pause(scope, paused)` | `pause_authority` or `governance` | `config` or `market` (mut) | Sets or clears `paused_opens` | SPEC §11: new opens **only** |
| `set_coverage_pause(paused, reason)` ★ | `coverage_authority` (A69; O17) | `config` (read), `market` (mut) | Sets or clears the market's `paused_coverage` flag (reason code `KeeperCoverage`); emits `CoveragePauseSet` | Inert while `coverage_authority == Pubkey::default()` (the default until O17). Opens only; moves nothing |
| `disable_venue(bit)` | `pause_authority` | `config` (mut) | Clears a `venue_enabled` bit; re-enabling is timelocked | Opens only |
| `flag_venue_upgraded(venue)` ★ | **anyone** | `venue_guard` (mut), that program's `ProgramData` | If `programdata.slot ≠ guard_slot[venue]` and the bit is clear: set bit `venue` in `upgraded`, record `flagged_slot`, `flagged_at`, emit `VenueUpgradeFlagged`. If the bit is set and `programdata.slot ≠ flagged_slot[venue]` (another upgrade during review, v0.4.1): update `flagged_slot`, `flagged_at`, emit `VenueUpgradeFlagged` again. Otherwise fail with `VenueNotUpgraded` (no write) | Address == the compile-time ProgramData constant, owner is the upgradeable loader, tag == 3. Replaces v0.3's per-open ProgramData read and the `VenueSlots` mirror (A7; decision 4) |
| `revalidate_venue(venue, expected_slot: u64)` ★★ | `pause_authority` | `venue_guard` (mut), that program's `ProgramData` | Requires `programdata.slot == expected_slot` (the deploy slot the Emergency signers reviewed), else `VenueSlotMismatch`; then `guard_slot[venue] = expected_slot`; clear bit `venue` | Same address and owner checks. Emits `VenueRevalidated{old, new}`. The expected slot stops an upgrade that lands while the Squads proposal collects signatures from being validated silently (R4B-7) |
| `set_route_broken(scope, venue_bit, broken)` | `pause_authority` or `governance` | `config` (venue bit) or `market` (mut) | Sets/clears `venue_route_broken` bit `venue_bit` (global scope) or `market.route_broken` (A40, C0-4 (b)) | **Built only after O6 is recorded in SPEC §14** (R3A-8 b). Moves no funds. Its only effects: opens refused, and `s3_no_route` for the S3 chain (§5.3). It is **not** an input to `venue_executable`, to any quote or leg, to close / liquidate / expire, or to `pool_fail` (V2-1, V1-7); T-S3-BROKEN proves it |
| `set_venue_extras(venue, ix, entries)` | `pause_authority` or `governance` | `config` (read), `venue_extras` (mut) | If the current set has stood for `EXTRAS_STABLE_S`, copies it to `stable` first (M2-SEC-2); moves the `current` set to `previous`, writes `entries` (≤ 4 fixed keys or derivation templates) as `current` with `version` + 1 and `current_since = now`; a **Governance** write also pins the new set as `stable` (A41, C0-4 (a), V2-6). Event `VenueExtrasSet{venue, ix, version, keys, stable_version}` | **Built only after O6** (built in M2). Fixed keys and template programs may not be short.win or the default key; templates have 1–4 seeds, constants ≤ 32 bytes, known placeholders (`BadExtras`); at use time each extra must be non-signer, not owned by short.win and not on the instruction's denylist (§2.4 rule 4; as built it also holds the market's `trader` and `trader_wsol`), which together keep every short.win-owned account and this market's own PDAs out. Callers may always select `none`, `previous` or `stable` |
| `sweep_protocol_fees()` | anyone | `config`, `market`, `market_vault` (mut), `fee_recipient` (mut, == config) | Moves `vault.protocol_fees` lamports to `fee_recipient`; sets it to 0. If the credit would leave the recipient below its rent-exempt minimum (a System account not yet funded), it moves nothing and keeps `protocol_fees` (event amount 0) until the fees or the recipient's balance cover it (M1-SEC-5 b) | The only debit of protocol money |

**What admins cannot do** (SPEC §9–§11; I4): move tokens out of `pool_vault`, move lamports out of `ShortPosition` or `MarketVault` (except the fee sweep to the configured recipient), change a position's locked terms, change a venue program id or venue PDA, re-point a market's mint, vaults or pool (pool changes only through the permissionless rule of §2.6), pause closes, liquidations, settlements, withdrawals or claims, or set any parameter outside its compiled bounds.

**Venue-upgrade latch (A7 as amended; ADOPTED Q13; masks v0.4.1).** Opens are refused while `venue_guard.upgraded` has a bit of the market's venue mask set: PumpCurve: pump + pfee + **pAMM** (a curve position exits on PumpSwap after graduation, and the straddle quote `B̂_str` prices the pAMM pool); PumpSwap: pAMM + pfee; RaydiumV4 and RaydiumCpmm: **AMM v4 + CPMM** (a fast path can move the market from one to the other while positions are open) (R4B-7). A Raydium listing waits for the same mask (M3-SEC-2); pool selection waits only for the candidate's own program (`propose_pool` and an adopting `execute_pool_switch`; a cancellation is never latched; M4-SEC-8, §2.3.2). The keeper subscribes to the six ProgramData accounts over Yellowstone and calls `flag_venue_upgraded` in the slot after it sees a change (§7.1); anyone else can too. The guarantee is therefore the v0.3 mirror's (opens stop within a watcher's reaction time after an upgrade, which takes effect one slot after deployment), with no per-slot writes and no 10 MiB ProgramData copy per open (C1-15, V1-11). The residual — an open that lands between the upgrade taking effect and the flag — is disclosed (§8.5).

**New Emergency powers (A40, A41; owner item O6)** move no funds and are disclosed. They are not "pause" or "fee parameters" in SPEC §9's sense, so they are built only after the owner records them as a SPEC §9 amendment (R3A-8 b); until then S3 on an interface break relies on reader failures (A63). What they can do, stated fully (V1-7): the route-broken flag refuses opens and starts the S3 chain, which after `s3_wait_s` lets *liquidatable or expired* positions settle in SOL at `min(V − K_s, FV)` (checked on the settlement box) instead of waiting for a buyback; the extras sets change the trailing accounts of venue CPIs, and a bad set degrades nothing because callers can pick `none`, the previous set or the `stable` set (M2-SEC-2).

The alternative the review offered, a short-timelock upgrade path for adapter patches (C0-4 (c)), is **not** adopted. Corrected rationale (V1-8): the 48 h Governance upgrade authority already exists and is disclosed (§8.5), so a second, faster path would not add a new *kind* of power; it would shorten the window in which users can react to an arbitrary code change from 48 h to hours. Adapter-level changes that can be data (additive accounts, including per-mint PDAs) are covered by the extras templates; everything else reaches S3 or waits for the 48 h upgrade.

The program upgrade authority (the Governance multisig, 48 h time_lock) can still replace the code; that is disclosed as a trust assumption (§8.5).

### 3.2 `create_market(venue_hint: u8, max_creation_fee: u64)`

**Signer:** `creator` (payer).

**Named accounts:** `config`; `market` (init, `["market", mint]`); `market_vault` (init); `pool_vault` (init, `token::mint = mint, token::authority = market, token::token_program = token_program`); `mint` (InterfaceAccount<Mint>); `trader` (Unchecked, seeds); `trader_base_ata` (Unchecked; created by CPI `create_idempotent` for pump venues, then validated); `token_program` (Interface); `associated_token_program`, `system_program`; `event_authority`, `program`.

**Remaining:** always the detection set `[bonding_curve PDA, canonical_pool PDA]` (both derived on-chain and key-checked, C0-15); then the init set of the detected venue:

- PumpCurve: pump program, pump UVA, pump event authority (for `init_user_volume_accumulator`);
- PumpSwap: pAMM program, pAMM UVA, pAMM event authority;
- `venue_hint = RaydiumV4 / RaydiumCpmm`: the candidate's ReadSet (§3.12), which must pass `eligible()` (§2.6) — at the candidate's own spot, since a new market has no `P_ema` (M4-SEC-3: the locked-depth floor, no live `R` or `λ` clause).

**Checks.**

1. `check_mint_safety(mint)`, a hard failure (ADOPTED Q7) [R-MINT §9.2]: owner ∈ {Tokenkeg, Token-2022}; not a native mint (`So111…112`, `9pan9bMn…`); `freeze_authority == None`; every TLV type ∈ {MetadataPointer, TokenMetadata, GroupPointer, TokenGroup, GroupMemberPointer, TokenGroupMember}; any parse error rejects.
2. `token_program.key == mint.owner`.
3. Venue detection (§2.5), with the D1 precedence and the ADOPTED Q6 rejections (mayhem mode, non-SOL quote). Curve accounts shorter than 115 bytes fail with `CurveNeedsExtend` (the client prepends pump `extend_account`) [R-PUMP §2.1]. PumpSwap pool accounts shorter than 300 bytes are allowed; readers zero-pad and clients prepend `extend_account` before trading [R-VEN §1.2].
4. `market_creation_fee ≤ max_creation_fee` (`CreationFeeAboveMax`; M1-SEC-11): the fee is read from `config` at execution and a timelocked change (≤ 0.1 SOL) can land between signing and execution, so the creator bounds it as `max_open_fee` bounds the open fee.
5. A Raydium hint is refused while `venue_guard` (read-only) blocks the Raydium mask (`VenueUpgraded`; M3-SEC-2): the listing fixes the pool, its SOL side and `graduated_at` with the compiled readers, so it waits for the Emergency review of an upgrade as opens do (§3.1). `Auto` listings are never latched.

**Effects.**

- Initialise `Market`: identity and bumps; own keys; `bonding_curve` and `canonical_pool` keys; the venue caches (curve ATAs with the **mint's token program** for base and Tokenkeg for WSOL [R-MINT §8.5]; `sharing_config = PDA(pfee, ["sharing-config", mint])`; UVA PDAs; creator vault from `curve.creator`; or the Raydium pool keys and SOL side); `venue`, `created_at = now`.
- Price state if the venue has a price: `P_ema = P_spot`, `L_ema = L_slow = √(X·Y)`, `ema_ts = slow_ts = now`, a ring with the one entry `(now, P, L, P, L, reprice(L, P))` and an empty bucket (ECON §5.2); **a Raydium listing** (PROPOSED, M4R, row 46) uses `Observation::new_raydium(now, P_spot, √(X·Y), cap)` instead: `L = min(√(X·Y), ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋)` and the state marked unconfirmed (`ema_warm = EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS`, `ema_guard = 1`), so the first open waits for the 8 warm-up integrations that re-seed `P_ema` from the pool's own buckets (§2.6 rule 1, §4.4); pump venues and the mock are unmarked; `share_epoch = 0`, `MarketVault.pi = WAD`, basis indices 0, `ema_params_version` and `kink_version` current.
- Age (A12, ADOPTED Q18; O13 extended by R3B-12): PumpSwap `graduated_at = now` (age from listing, conservative for a pool that graduated long ago); Raydium CPMM `graduated_at = open_time ≥ T_CLAMP ? min(now, open_time) : now` (a valid lower bound on pool age [R-RAY §7]; a pool opened before `T_CLAMP` ages from listing, O13; M3-SEC-4); Raydium AMM v4 `graduated_at = now` (`pool_open_time` is unusable [R-RAY §7]); Raydium markets set `pool_seen_at = now` (opens wait `pool_list_window_s`).
- Pump venues: CPI `ATA::create_idempotent(trader_base_ata)` (payer creator), then validate mint, owner and program; CPI `init_user_volume_accumulator(payer = creator, user = trader)` on the current venue's program, skipped if the UVA already exists (a griefer creating it first is harmless: it is keyed to `trader`) [R-PUMP §3.6].
- `tier` computed (§4.7).
- System-transfer `market_creation_fee` from the creator to `market_vault`; `vault.protocol_fees += fee`.

**Event:** `MarketCreated{…, fee}`. **Errors:** `MintUnsupportedProgram`, `MintNative`, `MintFreezeAuthority`, `MintDisallowedExtension`, `MintMalformed`, `CurveMayhemUnsupported`, `CurveQuoteNotSol`, `CurveNeedsExtend`, `VenueAccountMismatch`, `PumpVenuePrecedence`, `VenueUpgraded` (Raydium hint, M3-SEC-2), `PoolIneligible`, `PoolTooThin` (the locked-depth floor, M4), `CreationFeeAboveMax`.

Listing is permissionless and never needs a venue: a `None` market accepts deposits and refuses opens until a venue appears (§2.5). Cost to the creator: ≈ 0.0297 SOL of account rent on Raydium (`Market` 3,104 B: 16,418,560 lamports; `MarketVault` 2,200 B: 11,826,240; `pool_vault` 1,488,440) and ≈ 0.0326 SOL on pump venues (plus the trader ATA and UVA), plus the 0.005 SOL fee: ≈ 0.035–0.038 SOL in total (v0.4: 0.023–0.026; the difference is v0.4.1's 2 KB lock expiry calendar and the wider ring entries; SPEC §3 suggests ~0.02 SOL; ADOPTED Q24). A weekly calendar (16 buckets) would save ≈ 0.009 SOL per market but lengthen a lock by up to 7 days instead of 1. Rent-exempt minimum = `(len + 128)·5,080` lamports [V5].

### 3.3 Lender instructions

These implement `sim/pool_model.py` v0.4.1 exactly (`advance`/`_release`, `_vest`, `_credit`, `_credit_r`, `_cal_advance`, `_cal_add`, `_cal_sub`, `lock_until_for`, `_touch`, `_to_pot`, `_maybe_roll`, `_roll`, `_resolve`, `_lock_of`, `locked_total`, `free_allowance`, `deposit`, `withdraw`, `claim_sol`, `cover`, `sweep_sol`, `borrow`, `repay`, `settle`; ECON §9). Option (a) of the round-3 brief is the design (continuous rent entitlement, SPEC §2.4); option (b) is owner item O16 (§10). Where the state lives: `acc`, `acc_r`, `S`, `T`, `ΣB_Q`, `receivable`, `max_release`, the vest, the share epoch and the post-wipe sweep bits on `Market`; `lender_sol`, the pot `unmatched`, `protocol_fees`, the lock state (Π, `pi_epoch`, `lock_norm`, `b_min`) and the lock expiry calendar on `MarketVault` (§2.3.6).

**`credit(x, realised)`** (shared, pool_model `_credit`): if `S > 0`, `acc += ⌊(x·WAD + carry)/S⌋` (remainder in `carry`) and, if realised, likewise `acc_r`. If `S == 0`: if `wipe_live`, add `x·WAD/s_old` to the live full-wipe record (`acc_end`, and `acc_r_end` if realised; remainders in the record's carries; V2-12); **otherwise the SOL has no lender and goes to the protocol**: `lender_sol −= x`, `protocol_fees += x` (R3A-10; `unassigned_sol` is gone).

**`vest(x)`** (pool_model `_vest`; R3B-2, R4B-5): lumps — the lender half of a penalty, the expiry fee, the part of an S2/S3 take above `⌊Q·P_ema/WAD⌋ + R_due` (R4B-6), trader sweeps and venue cashback — are already in `lender_sol` and are released linearly: release what is due first; if `S == 0`, `credit(x, realised)`; else, if `vest_bal > 0 ∧ vest_end > now`, `vest_end = now + ⌈(vest_bal·(vest_end − now) + x·lump_vest_s)/(vest_bal + x)⌉` (value-weighted: a dust lump moves the end at most 1 s later, and no lump moves it earlier while `lump_vest_s ≥ vest_end − now`, i.e. unless governance lowered `lump_vest_s` below the remaining span), otherwise `vest_end = now + lump_vest_s`; then `vest_bal += x`, `vest_last = now`. v0.4 restarted the window for the whole balance on every lump, which 1-lamport sweeps could exploit (ECON §9.4). S1 SOL and the immediate part of an S2/S3 take are **not** vested.

**`touch(lender)`** (shared, pool_model `_touch`): for each share epoch the lender is behind, read the `ShareEpoch` record (remaining accounts, key-checked PDAs `["share_epoch", market, e]`), then `pending += shares·(acc_end − snap)/WAD`, `real += shares·(acc_r_end − snap_r)/WAD`, and:

- **Live wipe record (V2-12):** if that record is the live full wipe (`wipe_live`, `num = 0`), the lender stays on it (`snap = acc_end`, `snap_r = acc_r_end`), moves any surplus to the pot, and stops: it has no current shares and `withdraw` refuses.
- otherwise `shares = ⌊shares·num/den⌋`, `lock = ⌊lock·num/den⌋`, `snap = snap_r = 0`, `epoch += 1`; **jump (V2-12):** if that left `shares == 0 ∧ lock == 0`, set `epoch = market.share_epoch`, `snap = acc`, `snap_r = acc_r` at once.

Then `pending += ⌊shares·(acc − snap)/WAD⌋`, `real += ⌊shares·(acc_r − snap_r)/WAD⌋`, `snap = acc`, `snap_r = acc_r`, and:

- **to the pot (R3A-2):** `surplus = (real − claimed) − max(pending, 0)`; if `surplus > 0`, `real −= surplus` and `MarketVault.unmatched += surplus`. Realised SOL is allocated to the shares holding when it arrives, entitlement to the shares that held while it accrued; the surplus of a lender who joined late is exactly the deficit of one who left early, and the pot connects them without a division by `S − shares` and without a keeper duty.
- **lock expiry (R3B-1; v0.4.1):** if `lock > 0 ∧ now ≥ lock_until ∧ lock_epoch == pi_epoch`: `lock = lock_term = 0`; `lock_epoch = NONE`. The aggregate is **not** touched here: the calendar dropped the lock's bucket at `lock_until` (§2.3.6), so every other lender's allowance stopped counting it at that second, whether or not this lender is ever touched (R4A-2, R4B-3). The epoch conversion scales `lock_term` along with `lock`.

If a lender is behind by more epochs than fit in one transaction, `sync_lender` advances it first.

**`deposit(amount: u64, min_shares: u128)`**

- Signer `owner`. Accounts: `config`, `market` (mut), `market_vault` (mut), `lender` (init_if_needed, `["lender", market, owner]`), `mint`, `owner_token_account` (mut; mint and token program checked), `pool_vault` (mut), `token_program`, `system_program`, event accounts; `ShareEpoch` records if behind.
- Checks: mint guard (Entry); `amount > 0`; `minted > 0`; `minted ≥ min_shares`; `S + minted ≤ u128::MAX`.
- Effects: prologue steps 1–5 (vest release included); `touch`; `minted = ⌊amount·(S + VS)/(T + ΣB_Q + VA)⌋`; if `S == 0 ∧ wipe_live`: **require `wipe_trader_swept` and — on a pump venue that can execute — `wipe_rewards_claimed`, else `WipeSweepPending`** (R4A-7; both instructions are permissionless, so a depositor bundles them, and they credit the wipe record while `S == 0`), then clear `wipe_live` (the wipe record is final from now on) and `touch` again; `transfer_checked(owner → pool_vault)`; `T += amount`, `S += minted`, `shares += minted`.
- CPI Guard: a lender whose token account has `CpiGuard.lock_cpi` cannot deposit through our CPI; the frontend detects it. A delegate-based deposit path is v1.1 [R-MINT §8.4].

**`withdraw(shares: u128, min_tokens: u64, min_sol: u64)`**

- Signer `owner`. Accounts: as `deposit`, plus the optional `memo_program` (below).
- Checks (C0-9, C0-13): mint guard (Exit: never reverts on the filter); `pending ≥ 0` after `touch`, else `NegativePending` (use `cover_deficit`); the lender is on the current epoch; `shares ≤ lender.shares`; `tokens = ⌊shares·(T + ΣB_Q + VA)/(S + VS)⌋ ≤ T` and `≤ allowance`, with (ECON §9.3; `pool_model.free_allowance`)
  ```
  cal_advance(now)                                                           (§2.3.6; v0.4.1)
  lock_i(now) = 0 if now ≥ lock_until or lock_epoch ≠ pi_epoch, else min(⌈lock·Π/lock_pi⌉, shares_i)
  locked      = min(⌊lock_norm·Π/WAD⌋, S) ;  u = shares_i − lock_i(now)      (floor, v0.4.1)
  allowance   = min(⌊u·T/max(S − locked, u)⌋, ⌊shares_i·(T + ΣB_Q + VA)/(S + VS)⌋, T)
  ```
  and `tokens ≥ min_tokens`.
- **The last shares stay while anything is borrowed** (§9 M0b carried-over note 4; M1-SEC-3): a withdrawal that would leave `S = 0` while `ΣB_Q > 0` is refused (`LastSharesBorrowed`); the last lender can withdraw all but its last share.
- **`pay ≥ min_sol`** (`SolBelowMin`; M1-SEC-2), with `pay` the SOL part below: the caller bounds the SOL as `min_tokens` bounds the tokens. A dust deposit landed just before the last lender's full exit would make it "not last" and hand its unreleased vest to the depositor; the frontend sets `min_sol` to the quoted payout, vest included, so that front-run reverts.
- **Lock update**: `b_max = ⌈allowance·(S + VS)/(T + ΣB_Q + VA)⌉`; if `ΣB_Q > 0 ∧ b_max > 0 ∧ u > b_max`, `add = ⌈min(shares, b_max)·(u − b_max)/b_max⌉`, else `add = 0`; `new_lock = min(lock_i(now) + add, shares_i − shares)`; if `new_lock > 0 ∧ lock_norm == 0`, `b_min = ΣB_Q` (first lock of the Π epoch, V2-3); **if the current lock is live** (`lock_i(now) > 0`), remove its old term from its bucket `lock_until/86,400` and from `lock_norm` (an expired lock's bucket is already gone); `lock_term = ⌊new_lock·WAD/Π⌋` (floor, v0.4.1); **`lock_until = max(lock_i(now) > 0 ? lock_until : 0, ⌈(max(now, market.max_release) + lock_grace_s)/86,400⌉·86,400)` when `add > 0`** (unchanged otherwise); add `lock_term` to bucket `lock_until/86,400` and to `lock_norm`; `lock_pi = Π`, `lock_epoch = pi_epoch`.
- **Last lender (R3B-2, R3A-10):** if `shares == S` and `vest_bal > 0`, release the whole vest to the current shares first and `touch` again, so the leaving lender is paid it. **Any other exit forfeits the lender's unreleased vest to the lenders who stay** — to a dust holder if only dust stays (disclosed; R4B-4). A threshold flush ("release the vest before a withdrawal that leaves < 1 % of `S`") was rejected: it lets a whale that deposits 199× just before a lump take 99.5 % of it (ECON §9.4). The frontend warns before such an exit.
- Effects: `transfer_checked(pool_vault → owner, tokens)` with the market PDA signing; `S −= shares`, `T −= tokens`; then pay SOL exactly as `claim_sol`. This is SPEC §2.4's "pro-rata mix of tokens and SOL".
